# VALQUO_LEDGER.md — the one place that answers "where do we stand?"

One row per external-audit item (`valquo_audit_items.json`, 134 items). This
file replaces reconstructing project state from git history.

## The contract (three rules — read them, they are why this file exists)

1. **Every agent updates its rows as part of its handoff.** A landed item with
   no ledger row is not finished work — the same standing as the existing
   "code without a handoff entry is not finished work" rule in `RUN_RULES.md`.
2. **The ledger is the answer to "where do we stand."** If it cannot answer,
   the ledger is broken and *fixing the ledger* is the task. Never another
   archaeology dig.
3. **Rows are append-and-amend, never silently rewritten.** A status that
   changes keeps its history in the note (`was X (sha) -> now Y`), because this
   project has already been bitten by claims that quietly changed meaning.

## How to read a row

* **status** — `OPEN` / `IN PROGRESS` / `DONE` / `BLOCKED` / `SUPERSEDED`.
* **verdict** — only for items that were actually measured: `ADOPTED` /
  `REJECTED` / `NULL` / `INCONCLUSIVE` / `DEFERRED`. It is filled in **only when
  the write-up literally uses one of those five words.** Most of the B series
  concluded `FIXED`, and `X8` concluded `REPLICATES` — real outcomes, but not
  verdicts in this vocabulary, so their column is blank and the write-up's own
  word is quoted in the note instead. Blank therefore means *"not measured, or
  measured and reported in different words"* — never *"we don't know"*.
* **commit** — a sha, so any claim here is checkable in one step. It is the
  commit whose *subject names the item* where one exists; otherwise it is the
  commit that **introduced the write-up**. Many items landed inside multi-item
  commits ("eleven Part I corrections") that never name them, so for much of the
  B series this is *"where it was recorded"*, not *"where it was fixed"* — a
  weaker claim, and stated here rather than left to be assumed. Unfinished rows
  carry no sha at all: a commit that merely *mentioned* an item reads as
  evidence of work done, and is worse than a blank.
* **handoff** — where the real write-up lives. The ledger is an index, not a
  replacement for it.
* **src** — `auto` = mechanically proposed and not yet read by a person; treat
  as a lead, not a fact. **Anything else** (`human`, or a lane name such as
  `pipeline builder`) = hand-verified against the write-up; `build_ledger.py`
  will NOT overwrite it, only report a disagreement. The test is *"is it
  `auto`?"*, not *"is it `human`?"* — it used to be the latter, which quietly
  demoted every row a lane had signed with its own name.

## Four traps that already produced wrong counts — do not re-make them

1. **A forward reference is not a completion.** "feeds U1", "needed for S12",
   "(supports D1)" say the item is *wanted*, not *done*. Counting these is what
   produced the 68/134 figure against a header-only count of 38/134.
2. **`P1`–`P5` collide with the project's own PHASE labels.** CLAUDE.md's
   "DONE (P4 commit)" is phase P4; audit item P4 is open and explicitly "out of
   band". `P6`–`P10` and `P24.x` are phases only — the audit's P series stops
   at P5.
3. **`M2` is ambiguous across documents.** HANDOFF_STATUS.md's "the audit's M2
   (SanDisk/WDC)" is `CODE_AUDIT.md`'s M2. The external audit's M2 is
   "clustered inference default" and has never been touched.
4. **`D1`–`D10` collide with DECILE labels**, which this project writes
   constantly ("long-short (D1-D10)", "D1 22.8% → D10 10.7%").
5. **A multi-item commit subject donates one item's verdict to every other id
   in it.** `275e9af` reads *"O16/O24 RESULTS: O24 is a NULL; O16 stopped at
   its own reproduction gate"*. The word `NULL` is **O24's** verdict, but the
   subject names both, so the proposal marks **O16 DONE** — an item that
   deliberately returned no verdict at all. Found 2026-08-08.
6. **`DEFERRED` is in the DONE vocabulary, so "deliberately deferred" reads as
   finished.** B23's own heading is `## B23 — DEFERRED, deliberately` and its
   body opens *"Not done, and not forgotten."* The proposal says DONE.
   Found 2026-08-08.

`build_ledger.py` encodes traps 1–4. **Traps 5 and 6 are NOT encoded** — both
were left as reported disagreements rather than fixed, because the fix is a
guess about commit-subject grammar and the human row already wins. Re-check
them on every refresh.

## Ledger accuracy — checked against the tree 2026-08-07 (out-of-band claims audit)

The `CLAUDE.md` claims audit (`HANDOFF_claims_audit.md`, options-bot lane) verified every status
it touched against the commit the row names, not against prose. **The ledger came out well: every
status it checked — B6, B7, B8, B11, B12, B17, M1, R1, R9, R10, X2, X3, X7, X8, U7 — was
corroborated by the code or the artifact.** Two things worth carrying, neither edited here because
the rows are not this lane's:

* **`R3`'s note is stale.** It reads "Shrinks every options t ~1.36x". The corrected figure is
  **√2.212 = 1.487×** on the 3,885-trade book; 1.36× came from the pre-correction 3,042-trade book
  and `CLAUDE.md` already carries the correction. **Owner: whoever owns R3.**
* **`X8` is DONE and `CLAUDE.md` did not know it.** The ledger was right and the brief was wrong —
  which is rule 2 of the contract working exactly as intended. `CLAUDE.md` now records it.

**C6 closed 2026-08-07 (same lane, separate task) — and it is the one row the ledger got wrong,
in an instructive direction.** The row named a blocker requiring Don and the Oracle box
("must scp quant_bots/data/*.py off the box"). That blocker was never real: the missing sources
sat in `options-bot/handoff/quant_bots.zip`, **tracked in this repository the entire time**, and
were recovered byte-identical without touching the box — which is as well, since the box is now
decommissioned. **The lesson is narrow and worth stating: "the only copy is on machine X" is a
claim about where you looked, not about where the file is.** Nobody had grepped the tracked zips.
`options-bot/.gitignore:34` (`!handoff/*.zip`) is the line that saved the project, and it should
not be tidied away.

## Refresh of 2026-08-08 (r1 lane) — counts, and the three ways `--write` was destroying this file

**`--write` had never been run since the out-of-band rows were added, and running it would have
deleted eight of them.** The refresh was done only after fixing that. All three defects share one
signature — the script curating content it did not write — against a docstring promising the
opposite (*"It never silently overwrites a human-verified row"*):

1. **`render()` iterated the 134 audit ids, so any row without an audit id was DROPPED.** That is
   all eight out-of-band rows: `OOB1`/`OOB2`/`OOB3` and the project's own pre-registered
   experiments `LOO`, `SELRULE`, `HACFLOOR`, `MLPREREG`, `MLCOMB` — Sessions 7–11 and the public
   fair-value leak closure. `OOB1`'s note had been carrying the warning *"build_ledger.py
   regenerates from the 134 audit ids only and will DROP this row"*: the defect was **documented in
   the data instead of fixed**, so the next person to type `--write` would have lost the rows and
   the warning together.
2. **The preserve check was `src == "human"` exactly**, so the seven rows signed
   `src=pipeline builder` were treated as machine-generated and rewritten from the proposal —
   `B8` and `P4` lost their `FIXED` verdicts that way. Now anything that is not the literal
   `auto` is protected, so a new `src` spelling degrades to *protected*, never to *overwrite me*.
3. **`render()` emitted the hard-coded `LEDGER_HEADER`**, deleting any prose written under it —
   here the entire "Ledger accuracy" section below, i.e. R3's stale-figure note and the C6 lesson.
   The header is now read back from the file.

Pinned by `tests/test_build_ledger.py` (**20 assertions**, incl. a round-trip of the real file).
**After the fix the whole refresh moved one cell**: `D4`'s note, `no mention anywhere` →
`prose mentions only` (`HANDOFF_data_spend.md:161`, genuine). Status unchanged, still OPEN.

**The fix for defect 3 had a bug of its own, caught by its own test.** The first version split the
header at the first line starting with `|` — which, once this very counts table was added below,
truncated the header there and deleted every section after it. The boundary is now the table's
exact `COLS` header line. The test was strengthened at the same time: it had asserted only that
the header was non-empty and contained no table rows, **both true of a truncated header**, so it
now compares the whole prose block byte-for-byte.

**74 of 134 DONE (55.2%).** Hand-verified 93/134; 41 rows still `src=auto` (leads, not facts) —
and **every one of those 41 is OPEN** (S 24, O 14, M 2, D 1), so the auto rows are entirely
the untouched backlog rather than unreviewed claims. No `DONE` row anywhere rests on machine
evidence alone.

**THE STATUS COLUMNS DO NOT SPAN THE STATUSES IN USE, AND THE `U` ROW IS WHERE IT SHOWS.**
The `U` series is now 4 `DONE`, 1 `PARTIAL` (`U2`) and 3 `DESIGN-RECORDED` (`U4`, `U6`,
`U8`), totalling 8 — but this table has columns only for OPEN / IN PROGRESS / DONE /
BLOCKED / SUPERSEDED, so its `DONE` cell reads 4 and the remaining 4 rows appear nowhere.
Reported rather than resolved by rounding them into `DONE`: a `DESIGN-RECORDED` row has no
measurement behind it and a `PARTIAL` row has an untested half, so counting either as `DONE`
would tell a future session they had been tested. **The `U`-series rows are the authority;
this cell is a summary that cannot express them.**

**The backlog is lopsided, and the counts are what make it legible:** `S` (4/28 DONE) and `O`
(6/26) hold **43 of the 52 OPEN items**. What remains is overwhelmingly signal ideas and options
studies, not corrections — `B` 24/26, `C` 7/7, `D` 9/10, `P` 5/5 and now `U` (0 OPEN) are
essentially finished. The other 7 OPEN items are spread thinly across `M` (3), `X` (2),
`B` (1), `D` (1).

Plus **8 out-of-band rows** (7 DONE, 1 PRE-REGISTERED), counted separately and deliberately: they
are real work, but folding them in would silently change what "of 134" has always meant.

Landings of the last two days are all present and were verified individually rather than assumed:
`HACFLOOR` (session 10), `MLPREREG` + `MLCOMB` (session 11), `O16`, `O24`, `P2`, `C6`, `OOB1`
(the leak closure). **Every one had already been recorded by the lane that did it** — rule 1 of
the contract is being followed.

### Where sources disagree — 21 items, and the human row wins all 21

The proposal is mechanical and its asymmetry is deliberate (a wrongly-OPEN row costs a re-check;
a wrongly-DONE row stops work happening). Every disagreement below was checked against the
write-up. **No status changed.** Four causes:

| cause | items | why the human row is right |
|---|---|---|
| **Script proposes DONE, item is not done** | `B13`, `B23`, `O16` | The dangerous direction, and all three are false. `B13` is "PARTIALLY FIXED and labelled so" — `MIN_AVG_DOLLAR_VOLUME` still cannot bind. `B23` is trap 6. `O16` is trap 5 and **stopped at its own reproduction gate with no verdict**. |
| **Script proposes IN PROGRESS, item is DONE** | `B9`, `O2`, `U5`, `D8`, `D9`, `M5` | "section exists, no completion word found" — the write-ups conclude in words outside `DONE_CUES` (`RELABELLED`, `adopted: []`, "Protocol WRITTEN"). Vocabulary gap, not a status question. |
| **Script proposes OPEN, item is DONE** | `P2`, `P3`, `P5`, `D1`, `D2`, `D5`, `D6`, `D7` | The collision guards firing as designed. `P2` **has** headers (`HANDOFF_crowding.md:1`, `HANDOFF_STATUS.md:53`) but `P` is a colliding series and neither heading carries an audit cue. `D1`/`D2`/`D5`–`D7` are written up as **table rows** in `HANDOFF_data_spend.md`, not headings, so there is no HEADER evidence to find. |
| **Script proposes OPEN, item is BLOCKED** | `R4`, `R5`, `R6`, `R8` | Blocked on **lane ownership**, not on evidence: they sit in `valuation/edge/**`, which `AGENTS.md` assigns to the pipeline-builder lane. The script cannot see an ownership boundary. Defensible either way; kept as BLOCKED because that is what a reader needs to know. |

**One row is stale and is still not this lane's to edit:** `R3`'s note reads *"Shrinks every
options t ~1.36x"*; the corrected figure is **√2.212 = 1.487×** on the 3,885-trade book (1.36×
came from the pre-correction 3,042-trade book). Carried forward from the 2026-08-07 audit below,
unedited for the second refresh running. **Owner: whoever owns R3.**

Reported not buried: quality (-0.12) and momentum (+0.88) do NOT generalise to Japan, so the composite replicates while its composition does not; only 5 of 7 themes map (insider/institutional have no analogue); JKP earns +2 to +3.4%/yr vs Valquo's +20.4% long-short, a factor of six on a different instrument, so this corroborates the PREMIA and NOT the magnitude. Measured on the panel it takes a mean of 1.25 DISTINCT VALUES PER DATE, with a median within-date standard deviation of 2.054 days, because 13F quarter-ends are COMMON CALENDAR DATES - so at any rebalance every name's 13F is the same age. Its decay multiplier therefore spans p05 0.5163 to p95 0.5427 with a WITHIN-DATE standard deviation of 0.00587, which means arm A4 is not a staleness adjustment at all but a UNIFORM 0.54x DOWN-WEIGHTING of the institutional theme - a weight change, and the weighting family was rejected wholesale the previous session. Its rank correlation against the deployed composite is 0.9880, the highest of the four arms and nearly inert, which is the same fact seen from the other side. THE AUDIT'S PREMISE CONFLATES TWO DECAYS: the 13F signal genuinely decays as the quarter ages, which is measured and real, but that is a TIME-SERIES decay common to every name, not a CROSS-SECTIONAL one that could re-rank them. THE FINDING THAT REFRAMED THE TASK, and it corrects THIS ROW: the note below says the band is ALREADY LIVE in the taxable configuration so an adopt would change the DEFAULT rather than introduce the band. That is FALSE, and it was verified false before the register was written. exit_frac was consumed live in exactly three places and NONE of them applied it - valquo_index.py:306 wrote it into the payload as metadata, :358 printed it in a CLI banner, and web/app.py:331 displayed it. The only code that had ever APPLIED a band was the backtest. build_index was a pure top-N selection with no band and no notion of a held name, and valquo_index.py:298 said so in a comment: the band "requires the PREVIOUS book, so it is applied at rebalance time, not in this snapshot", emitted as an instruction for a human rebalancer rather than executed. So this adoption WIRED THE BAND INTO LIVE CONSTRUCTION FOR THE FIRST TIME, the taxable config's declared 20 pct band had never affected a book anyone received, and the fidelity gate was load-bearing rather than ceremonial. THE GATE PASSED, and it is provably not vacuous. The rule moved to valuation/edge/no_trade_band.py and BOTH callers import it, so fundamental_panel._band_select IS no_trade_band.band_select - one code object, not two equivalent implementations, pinned by an identity test. The live entry point build_index was then applied to the panel's most recent cross-section and reproduced the measured arm's book NAME-FOR-NAME: 184 names, identical set AND identical order, and 0 mismatches across ALL 69 dates. Non-vacuity is asserted rather than hoped for - with no held set both paths collapse to plain top-N and the comparison proves nothing, so the band is CHAINED across all 69 dates exactly as turnover_and_costs chains it, and at the gate date the banded book differs from plain top-N by 65 of 184 names with 65 retained; the run ABORTS if that is not so. Exact composite ties at the gate date: 0, so no argsort ambiguity. WHERE IT APPLIES AND WHERE IT DELIBERATELY DOES NOT: S14 measured the DECILE book, and exit_frac is a fraction of the ranked UNIVERSE, which is meaningful for a decile book and not for a fixed-N one - on a 25-name book against a large universe it would hold nearly every name nearly forever, and the panel's own comment says exit_mult is the only band meaningful for a fixed-N book. So taxable moves 0.20 to 0.30 and roth stays BAND-LESS, because a banded top-25 book is a construction nobody has measured and shipping one would borrow S14's evidence for it. VINTAGE 4 OPENED 2026-08-13, DERIVED AND NOT ASSUMED per PT-GAPDUE: current_vintage() returned vintage 3 opened 2026-08-11, and the FIDELITY-2 amendment clause is explicitly self-limiting - it amends in place only at ZERO accrued complete days and stops applying the moment one complete day accrues. Vintage 3 had accrued TWO, so the clause did not apply, Amendment 1's ordinary rule resumed and vintage 4 opened. Rule 6 is paid in full and not hidden: the accrued forward clock resets to zero and vintage 3's two days are spent. THE FIRST SHADOW PAIR IS NOW OPEN - V1 shipped as instrumentation with no pair to measure, which was the point, and the pair 4 over 3 is live with vintage 3's parameters pinned two days before this adoption was known about, so the predecessor is a genuine snapshot rather than a reconstruction. no_trade_band was added to PARAM_KEYS - pre-registered this time, unlike themes_scored_live - because without it vintages 3 and 4 hash IDENTICAL, the band changing no weight and no theme, and same_model would report no change while the book demonstrably changed; EXACTLY ONE KEY separates the pair, which is the honest description of this adoption as a change to selection and not to scoring. Vintage 3's published params_id stays 24878e43a1e3 and vintage 2's stays 0060c5ef3dda - neither is retroactively rewritten - and vintage 4's pin is a LITERAL 0.30 rather than an import of the constant, because a pin records what a vintage WAS and importing it would let a future width change rewrite vintage 4's history AND make vintages 4 and 5 hash identical. DISPLAY HONESTY: a retained name carries "held - challenger within band" in the why-attribution at row level and in the owner index block, the derived vintage_label now reads "Book vintage 4 since 2026-08-13 (no-trade band, width 0.30); vintage 3 runs in shadow", and a new headline_scope block states that the published backtest figures were measured WITHOUT a band - the method string is UNCHANGED and still describes the validated composite, because S14's evidence is a held-out DIFFERENCE and not a re-measured level. TWO FURTHER DIVERGENCES FOUND AND FIXED, both the same disease: /api/valquo-index and unified._index_membership each rebuilt the book with NO band while publishing a config block advertising one, so they would have served a DIFFERENT book from the exported Index under the same name; both now apply the same band through the same imported rule, and the config block itself is built in ONE place because export() and the route each carried their own copy of band_note - one would have been corrected and the other left telling readers to apply the band by hand, after which it would have run twice. REPORTED, NOT GATED AND NOT FIXED: the live book-size rule differs from the panel's - live rounds and floors at MIN_NAMES, the panel truncates - so they disagree on 32 of 69 dates. That predates the band and is not something S14 changed, so it is measured and recorded rather than folded into this adoption. ALSO NOT DONE: the measured figures on the taxable config were measured at width 0.20 and are NOT restated, since no run has measured that config at 0.30; they now ship beside an explicit measured_width so the two cannot be conflated. SIX TESTS ELSEWHERE HAD FROZEN A VINTAGE NUMBER and failed on a legitimate vintage event - including one named test_no_fourth_vintage_was_opened, which was a true statement about FIDELITY-2's own day rather than an invariant. All were rewritten to pin the DERIVATION rather than the literal, the lesson this codebase had already learned once; one of them, a gap-report test, had been rendered VACUOUS rather than red, because the date it skipped became the new inception and stopped matching any row. Equity N unchanged - an adoption searches nothing. 37 new tests; full gate 67 suites, 0 failures. | S28 | S | Distribution, not just the mean | **DONE - SHIPPED** | Four additive distribution blocks in the payload, SCHEMA_VERSION 5 to 6, reporting only and pinned as such |  | HANDOFF_edge_audit.md session 30 | 2026-08-12 | auto | REPORTING INFRASTRUCTURE: no hypothesis, no threshold, no verdict, and NO PUBLISHED CLAIM MOVES. Every book statistic this project ships is a mean or a t on a mean, and a mean is the one summary that cannot show a book being carried by a handful of quarters. New statistics.distribution() returns n, mean, sd, min, p05, p25, median, p75, p95, max, the COUNT and FRACTION of negative periods, and the DATED worst and best period. Wired into four payload blocks: construction.top_decile_alpha_distribution, construction.long_short_distribution, portfolio.return_distribution and portfolio.excess_vs_equal_weight_distribution. WHAT IT SHOWS ON THE SHIPPED BOOK, and this is why the item was worth doing: the published plus 7.17 pct per year top-decile alpha is a mean of 69 quarterly draws of which TWENTY ARE NEGATIVE, 28.99 pct, with a worst quarter of minus 6.83 pct on 2016-01-20 and a best of plus 11.47 pct on 2022-07-22, and a MEDIAN of plus 1.41 pct against a MEAN of plus 1.79 pct - so the headline is right-skewed and the typical quarter is worse than the average one. The long-short spread is negative in 33.3 pct of quarters, worst minus 20.01 pct on 2025-07-29. THE UNITS ARE CARRIED IN THE BLOCK because the obvious misuse is annualising a quantile: top_decile_alpha is periods-per-year times the MEAN, and that scaling is a statement about a mean, never about an order statistic. CONSISTENCY IS ASSERTED, NOT ASSUMED: four times the distribution's mean reproduces top_decile_alpha to 4e-17 on the real 69-date panel and exactly on a synthetic one, which is the check that the distribution describes the SAME series the headline is a mean of - a block attached to the wrong series would look reasonable and quietly mislabel the worst quarter in the record. PINNED AS REPORTING-ONLY by a test that fails if any threshold, gate or verdict ever compares or branches on a distribution field, and that guard was checked for vacuity - it inspects 14 code-level references, so it is not passing by seeing nothing. The dated extremes are matched against the ORIGINAL series rather than the cleaned one, because pairing a cleaned value with an uncleaned date is how an off-by-one mislabels the worst quarter. M6's field-level schema guard covers all four blocks. Zero equity trials - infra n=1 on the M2/M6 precedent, and infra N gates no published claim |

| U8 | U | One risk budget across books | DESIGN-RECORDED | premise CONFIRMED, prescribed method refuted - a drawdown optimiser returns a corner |  | DESIGN_u4_u6_u8.md | 2026-08-13 | human | DESIGN-RECORDED 2026-08-13. No hypothesis, no threshold, ZERO trials. Memo at DESIGN_u4_u6_u8.md. THIS ROW'S src=auto NOTE WAS WRONG - it read 'no mention anywhere in the corpus' and VALQUO_EDGE_AUDIT.md:2123 is a full U8 section. THE AUDIT'S CENTRAL EMPIRICAL CLAIM IS NOW MEASURED AND IT IS CORRECT: it argues that a volatility event firing many options alerts is also the event in which the equity book draws down and that nothing accounts for it. O11 measured that alerts cluster (median 7 per week, max 38, 51.5% of trades in weeks of more than 10) and U3 measured that those are the same episodes (correlation +0.4371; sleeve -84.39% in the equity book's worst quarters; COVID 2020Q1 equity -28.09% against sleeve -76.14%). WHAT THE AUDIT DID NOT ANTICIPATE IS THE DIRECTION: its framing at :2127 imagines a CONCENTRATION problem, more exposure during the drawdown. The measured problem is different in kind - the sleeve loses roughly THREE TIMES what it was supposed to be diversifying. THE PRESCRIBED METHOD AT :2129 CANNOT BE RUN AS WRITTEN, for three reasons in ascending severity. (1) The allocation it would produce is already known and is a CORNER: U3 swept exactly this allocation over X in 90..99 at two concurrency caps, 20 cells, and combined max drawdown was worse than equity alone in EVERY ONE, improving monotonically toward X=100, so a drawdown-minimising optimiser puts ZERO in the sleeve. (2) Ledoit-Wolf shrinks a covariance matrix toward a structured target and on a 2x2 there is essentially nothing to shrink - sophistication as decoration. (3) The drawdown estimate it would optimise rests on ONE episode: S10 measured the worst peak-to-trough as exactly one 63-day period at trough index 44 of 69, and U3 counts 5 equity drawdown episodes deeper than 5% on the covered subsample but only one of severity, so allocating on a contribution-to-drawdown estimate fitted to one crash fits a portfolio to a single observation - the in-sample selection this project already paid for at +8.43%/yr in-search to -0.04%/yr locked hold-out. WHAT IS BUILDABLE is smaller and more useful than an optimiser: one stated capital base, a concurrency-aware combined exposure line from O11's marks, a PRE-COMMITTED POLICY cap on the sleeve's share rather than a fitted one (U3 says the fitted answer is zero and the Sharpe-maximising answer is disqualified by the audit's own bar), and a tripwire when the alert rate enters O11's top decile of weeks. Reporting and policy, no new research, owned by the app/portfolio lane. Re-open the optimiser only if a genuinely long-vol sleeve exists to allocate to - which per U3 the current one is not. |

CAUSE: THE TWO SWEEPS RAN AT DIFFERENT PROJECT TRIAL COUNTS, AND N MOVES ls_t. cpcv_validate's adopt gate is (med[best]-med[default]) > _trials_haircut(len(names))*se, and _trials_haircut (fundamental_panel.py:2097) is FLOORED AT THE RESEARCH LOG'S N (audit M1). X7 ran at N=84 (haircut 2.97685), session 10 at N=121 (haircut 3.09703); scripts/placebo.py then feeds the ADOPTED weights to quantile_backtest, so adoption is monotone decreasing in N and a draw that stops adopting is re-scored under different weights. SEED 1005: margin 0.00287097 vs se 0.00094470, so it clears the N=84 bar of 0.0028122 and fails the N=121 bar of 0.0029257 - scored at naive ls_t 2.1273 under the challenger's weights and 1.0454 under base. Session 10's retained artifact records 1.0453572947436582, IDENTICAL to this session's base-weight recomputation to sixteen digits. Substituting the adopted value into session 10's 100 draws gives EXACTLY 8 at t>=2.0 (X7's figure), the adopt count at N=84 comes back 21 (M1's recorded 21%), and the naive p95 stays 2.1437 with max 3.436 - which is why session 10's control reproduced X7's percentiles to the digit while missing one draw: 2.1273 lands just below the 95th percentile. It also explains why it looked undiagnosable - seed 1005 did not drift across 2.0, it JUMPED 1.08 of a t because its weights changed, so "no draw near the boundary" was the wrong thing to look for. CONSEQUENCE THAT OUTLIVES IT: THE CALIBRATED PLACEBO FLOORS ARE FUNCTIONS OF N and nobody knew; here they happened not to move because the affected draw landed below the percentile, which is luck not design. Every future sweep must record the N it ran at, and a floor may not be compared across sweeps at different N without checking. The SHIPPED strategy is unaffected - it does not adopt, it keeps current-default, so no haircut touches its ls_t; the exposure is to the CALIBRATION, not the headline. Instrumentation: cpcv_validate now banks adopt_detail (margin, se, haircut, n_trials_used, folds_positive) and challenger_weights_cols - the challenger's weights WHETHER OR NOT adopted - so "what would this run have scored one haircut lower" is arithmetic. Zero trial cost. scripts/x7_reconcile.py; data/free_analysis/X7_RECONCILE.json retains all 100 rows. |

| PT-VINTAGE | X | The vintage rule - what closes a vintage, what does not, and what a verdict is about | **DONE** | ADOPTED 2026-08-09 |  | HANDOFF_edge_audit.md session 15 | 2026-08-09 | pipeline builder | Contract section 5a, implemented as track_meter.VINTAGES with current_vintage(). Any ADOPTED change to scoring, weights or construction closes the current vintage and opens the next; ADOPTED means it ships in the live scoring path, not proposed or tested. REBALANCING UNDER UNCHANGED RULES IS NOT A VINTAGE EVENT. Each vintage carries its own clock (own inception, own 6-month gate, own 60-month verdict); the gate and the meter attach to the CURRENT vintage and are computed on that window alone, so a verdict is a statement ABOUT A VINTAGE and must name it. The cross-vintage chain is kept and published as 'the system as operated' - the honest answer to what a user would have experienced - and is explicitly NOT the object of a contract verdict because it mixes models; as_operated() carries that disclaimer in its own payload and a test asserts the disclaimer names both 'model' and 'vintage'. RULE 6 IS THE BRAKE: a vintage change resets the whole accrued clock and buys nothing statistically, so anyone shipping a scoring change should know it costs the clock. Two construction points that took a rewrite each: a later vintage is BASELINED AT ITS OPENING LEVEL, not at zero, because the recorded series is cumulative since run #1 and zero-baselining would fold run #1's drift into vintage 2's first month; and as_operated uses RAW ENDPOINTS rather than complete months, because vintage 1 ran six days and holds no complete calendar month, so a month-based leg reported 0.0% for a window that actually moved -2.85pp. |

FIXED, four parts: (1) bound_series() gathers from all three places the series can live (committed backup, data/ tracker files, live store meta) and merge_bound_rows() unions them by date, later source wins a shared date and NO date is ever dropped, so a legitimately-empty source can never erase a populated one; (2) it is written as its own valquo_index_track.csv + valquo_index_meta.json, using the tracker's OWN column names so a restore is a copy and not a transformation; (3) guard_counts() reports bound_index_days and the workflow fails on a regression in EITHER book plus an ABSOLUTE presence check (a relative guard cannot catch "always zero"); (4) the emitted README no longer labels paper_track_index.csv "daily Valquo Index vs SPY" -- that exact mislabel put a false "Index beating SPY" claim into Discord on 2026-08-05 on a day the bound recorder had the track 2.85pp BEHIND. THE 2 BOUND ROWS ARE NOW COMMITTED (2026-07-31 -0.2777pp, 2026-08-06 -2.8468pp, 86 names) so the record no longer depends on one laptop; data/ STAYS gitignored and untouched -- the licensed-Sharadar rule is unchanged and the bound series is Valquo's own 127-byte derived output, committed to the already-tracked data_export/. IT IS A BACKUP, NOT A SECOND RECORDER: index_track.load() still reads data/ and only data/, so index_track.vs_spy_claim() remains the single authority. VERIFIED by running the workflow's steps against the payload the CURRENT pre-LA2 live service actually returns (no bound_index_track key at all): render exit 0, guard committed=2 new=2, artifact contains both bound rows; negative control with no committed copy correctly warns and fails the presence check. New tests/test_track_export.py (18 tests, the module had NONE) incl. a real restore through index_track.load() reproducing the published -2.8468pp. BUG FOUND IN MY OWN FIX, by its own test: recording the write-time merge count inside the artifact made two runs of identical input differ, breaking the byte-idempotence the module had promised in prose since it was written and never tested; the count moved to the run log. NOT DONE: nothing ingests the bound series into the live service's store (the Cowork-side writer LA2 does not cover), so on a fresh Render disk the API still serves an empty live column until the files are restored -- and there is still no automated writer, which is the operational gate's real blocker. | V3-COPY | V | V3's calibration verdict reaches the product - the score stops claiming per-name precision | **DONE** | SHIPPED 2026-08-10 |  | HANDOFF_appfixes.md session 22 | 2026-08-10 | app-fixer | THE DEPENDENCY V3 LEFT OPEN. `HANDOFF_extensions_v3.md` (lane r1, pre-registered blind at 251c989) closes with "Did not weaken the product's confidence language in the app ... the templates are the app-fixer's lane. This is an open dependency, not a finished item." This row is that item. V3's pre-registered PRIMARY statistic FAILED: composite at rank 10 reads 1.0909 against a noise p95 of 1.1117, empirical p 0.116 - roughly one chance-assembled universe in nine reaches the real value at that rank. Verdict NOT DISTINGUISHABLE, and it GENERALISES on 45 of 69 dates against a pre-registered gate of 42. LABELS AND COPY ONLY; no score computation changed. ONE SOURCE FOR THE WORDING: new `valuation/web/score_confidence.py`, every sentence appearing VERBATIM in HANDOFF_extensions_v3.md and pinned by a test that normalises the markdown and fails if EITHER side is reworded - r1's wording was written to survive scrutiny and a surface that tidies a calibrated hedge is how it becomes a claim again. Four surfaces read it: the hot-list legend (server-rendered directly over the ranked table), the discovery blurb ("a coarse ordering, not a precise one"), the per-name why-this-score panel in app.js, and /methodology's "Where it is weak" section. THE CALIBRATION PRINTS ABOVE THE ATTRIBUTION BARS, not under them: a caveat after the evidence reads as a footnote, before it, it frames how the bars are read. PER_NAME IS AN EXACT SUBSTRING OF DEFENSIBLE, not a shorter rewrite, asserted by a test - otherwise the name row and the legend become two independently-editable statements of one limit and a reader on a name row gets the softer of the two. app.js HOLDS NO COPY of the wording (reads window.SCORE_CONFIDENCE; a test asserts the sentences are ABSENT from the static file) - the same rule PT-OUTBOUND landed for outbound figures. Injected by the SHARED context processor, not per route, because index.html is rendered by BOTH web/app.py and saas/app_saas.py and the second place being forgotten is this project's recurring defect. THE SCOPE LINE IS THE PART THAT TOOK CARE: V3 settles the SCORE's precision and explicitly NOT the backtested return spread ("a composite can rank names in an order that is indistinguishable from chance at a given rank and still have a real top-minus-bottom return spread"). So the recency caveat attaches to the top decile's SCORE versus a chance book, NOT to top-decile alpha, the long-short HAC t of 2.620 vs its 2.28 floor, or R1's factor alpha; the top-decile mentions in methodology.html and portfolio.html that describe RETURNS were deliberately left alone, and a test fails if a return figure appears inside the calibration block. THE GROUP HALF NEVER TRAVELS ALONE: V3's flattering finding (top decile beats a chance book AS A GROUP, p 0.008) holds on only 21 of 69 dates, so a test walks every rendered page and fails if the group claim appears without its recency caveat in the same block; the caveat is stated as a BARE COUNT OF DATES, never a rate, because 21 of 69 overlapping cross-sections of largely the same names are nowhere near 21 independent draws (session 9: 16 co-moving countries were worth 2-4 draws and a bar with a claimed alpha of 3.84% measured out at 28.7%). PINS MUTATION-TESTED, NOT ASSUMED: four mutations - legend softened to "only weakly distinguishable", robustness count inflated 45->60, group caveat stripped of its date count, thin-data finding reworded - ALL FOUR CAUGHT; plus a non-vacuity test proving the markdown normaliser does not collapse distinct sentences into a match. 14 new tests (tests/test_score_confidence.py); 34 suites, 1036 passed, 0 failed. BUGS FOUND: (1) two copy seams spliced a quoted sentence mid-clause and read ungrammatically - found by RENDERING the page and reading it as text, not by the tests, which passed throughout because a substring pin cannot see grammar; (2) a first cut PARAPHRASED r1's "a specific rank, or the gap between #3 and #12, means anything" into my own words on /methodology - the exact drift this task exists to prevent, committed while writing the guard against it - now quoted and pinned as NO_LONGER_SAYABLE; (3) /work claims "628 tests across 19 suites" against a measured 1036 across 34, FLAGGED NOT FIXED (outside a score-copy scope, wants session 21's treatment of the trial counts); (4) /methodology's Deflated Sharpe copy is STILL stale since M1 (calls it undeflated and saturating at >99.9%; it is a genuine DSR of 0.8556 at N=129 failing the >0.95 convention while above all 100 placebo draws) - carried over from session 21, still OPEN, wants a paired copy+test change. NOT DONE: V3's finding 3, the thin-coverage tilt (real top-decile present weight 0.94798, only 9 of 500 noise draws that thin, p 0.018) - the product now DISCLOSES it but does not CORRECT it; a minimum-coverage floor or a variance penalty is r1's recommended next step and is an edge-lane pre-registration, not an app change. |
| V2F | V | Rate-limit-tolerant live-input coverage (V2 follow-up) | DONE | beta 46/403 -> 500/500, 0 throttles; 43pct of live weight inert |  | HANDOFF_live_data_bugs.md Part 12 | 2026-08-10 | human | V2 FOLLOW-UP, rate-limit-tolerant live-input path. Pre-registration committed ALONE at 1867a3f before scripts/live_cache.py existed. THE BRIEF'S PREMISE WAS HALF FALSE AND THE PRE-REGISTRATION SAYS SO BEFORE THE NUMBERS: the beta source really was at 46/403 on Yahoo throttling (runs 1 and 2 died at 176 and 297 throttled calls), but the theme-health meter was at 0/403 for an unrelated reason - a checkout never receives a real scan - so a tolerant fetch path fixes the first and cannot fix the second. RESULT: beta coverage 46/403 (11.4%) -> 500/500 (100.0%) with ZERO throttle events. What made it affordable is BATCHING not patience: yf.download pulled 500 monthly close series in 13 requests in ~40 seconds; only the vendor beta field (.info) cannot be batched, so that leg is paced at 2.5s. Resume follows mine_options_cache's tri-state rule - a failed or throttled unit is NOT recorded, so coverage cannot be inflated by running into a quota wall. The fetch and the measurement are now separate programs: report makes ZERO network calls and drives the REAL _resolve_beta ladder against the cache, which is the structural fix for 'a measurement that consumes the resource it is measuring'. RUNG DISTRIBUTION over 500: vendor 432 (86.4%), vendor_corroborated 31, fallback 22 (4.4%), computed 14, vendor_uncorroborated 1. MY PRE-REGISTERED PREDICTION WAS WRONG (60/40): I expected a HIGHER fallback share than the 46-name sample's 10.9%; it is LOWER at 4.4%, because that sample was enriched with problem names by construction (7 named cases + 5 deliberately out-of-band). B1 do-no-harm HELD on real cached data through an entirely different fetch path: GILD 0.305, CI 0.288, KSPI 0.886 (n=30), CHTR 0.669 all reproduce Part 7.6 exactly. THE FINDING: applying the coverage rule to the LIVE product, three themes carrying 0.375 of the 0.875 deployed weight mass - 42.9% - contribute NOTHING to any live score. capital_discipline and institutional are null on 500/500 served rows; insider is 100% non-null with exactly ONE distinct value. The hot list is a four-theme book (value, quality, size, momentum) wearing the weights of a nine-theme one. insider's deadness is documented at screen.py:288; the other two are not, and capital_discipline has the second-strongest backtest IC (+2.76). SECOND DEFECT, IN MY OWN V2 METER, AND THE DANGEROUS DIRECTION: every V2 coverage floor counts NON-NULL ROWS, so a constant theme passed all seven; and _spearman does not return NaN on a constant predictor, it returns an arbitrary number (roughly [-0.15,+0.17] vs random targets, EXACTLY +1.0 vs a monotone one). The meter would have banked those as genuine monthly observations and the anytime-valid band would eventually have called it significant - the absent themes always refused safely, this one would have produced a verdict. Fixed with a per-date degeneracy floor (MIN_DISTINCT_VALUES=2), recorded as a TIGHTENING permitted by PREREG_v2_theme_health section 10. Also fixed: the cache did not survive its own round trip (5y of monthly closes straddle DST, so str() serialisation emits mixed UTC offsets pandas refuses to parse); and the silent trap that motivated B1 - batched yf.download returns a tz-NAIVE index against a tz-AWARE market proxy, giving an empty intersection, compute_beta 'unavailable', and rung 3a keeping the vendor beta, i.e. corroboration silently disabled on every name with nothing raised. THEME LEG: 0 usable rows -> 500 real rows at scan_date 2026-08-08 via the public credential-free /api/hotstocks, replayed through the project's own Store.save_snapshot into a DEDICATED db (never data/screener.db). B4 HELD - not one of ten verdicts changed; B5 HELD - still zero closed 63d windows. What changed is the REASON: seven themes moved from 'empty in the record' to '0 closed monthly windows', i.e. from blocked by absent data to blocked by elapsed time. At 500 names a live IC of +0.0379 is detectable by month 60 vs quality's backtested +0.0356, confirming V2's calibration from the other direction. The endpoint serves only the LATEST scan, so the record accrues FORWARD one day per run and can never be backfilled - 9 real dates exist on Render that this repo cannot reach. 1199 tests across 26 suites, 0 failures. Zero trial cost; equity N stays 129. 5 BUGS FOUND. |

PREREG_v2g_live_theme_cost.md committed ALONE at 6d8750a before scripts/live_theme_cost.py existed and before any arm was scored. TWO PRE-SPECIFIED ARMS AND NO SELECTION STEP, so no decide/measure split is needed: A7 = the deployed seven themes at 0.125 each; B4 = value quality momentum size, the four that reach a live score. THE RESTRICTED ARM IS THE LIVE BOOK RATHER THAN A MODEL OF IT, and that is proved not asserted: C1 (three dead themes NaN) and C2 (insider CONSTANT, the other two absent - the live condition exactly) both reproduce the B4 composite name for name at max|dev| 0.000e+00 over all 113,945 rows, because composite renormalises by the PRESENT-weight mass and zscore returns all-NaN on a zero-variance column. HARNESS CONTROL: the incumbent arm reproduces the record to the digit - alpha 0.071741423321, LS naive t 2.8361, LS HAC t 2.6199, alpha HAC t 4.3762, monotonicity -0.891, equal-weight benchmark +18.137%. RESULT: B4 alpha +5.86% against A7's +7.17%, d_alpha -1.3133pp against the -1.95pp bar, paired HAC t -1.4040 over 69 paired dates, so the pre-registered verdict is IMMATERIAL and building the live sources is NOT the project's highest-value work. THE POWER CAVEAT MUST TRAVEL WITH IT: the HAC se of the paired annual difference is 0.9354pp so the design resolves 1.8708pp at abs(t)=2 - well matched to its own bar - but power against a TRUE 1.95pp gap is only 55.0%. IMMATERIAL means the cost could not be separated from zero at roughly a coin flip's power, NOT that it was shown to be small. THE SECOND FINDING IS MORE SERIOUS AND THE REGISTER ASKED FOR IT SEPARATELY: the live four-theme book FAILS the calibrated long-short floor, HAC t 1.8811 against 2.2837 and naive 2.0044 against 2.1437, where the deployed book clears at 2.6199. It DOES clear the top-decile alpha floor at 3.2087 against 2.2913, and since the shipped product is a LONG-ONLY hot list that is the product-relevant statistic - so the long-only book users receive stays demonstrable while the long-short figure quoted beside it does not. BOTH SPLIT DIRECTIONS AGREE IN SIGN ON ALPHA AND NEITHER IS SIGNIFICANT (early -1.14pp at t -1.3519, late -1.48pp at t -0.8984), a better stability profile than session 7's LOO arms, four of seven of which changed sign. REPORTED BECAUSE IT CUTS AGAINST THE VERDICT: the late-half LONG-SHORT difference is -5.63pp/yr at HAC t -2.0639, which crosses the conventional bar - not the pre-registered primary statistic, uncalibrated, and one of six cells, but a reader gets it rather than only the agreeing cells. EXPLORATORY DECOMPOSITION, NO VERDICT, registered as carrying none because session 7 established on this exact panel that a full-sample ablation arm is not a finding: dropping institutional is the ONLY one negative in both halves (-1.41% full, -0.89% early, -1.91% late), so 13F is the source to build first; dropping capital_discipline is POSITIVE in both halves (+1.37% full) despite it holding the second-strongest panel IC (+2.76), a restatement of X3's finding that theme IC does not predict marginal contribution; dropping insider flips sign between halves. WHAT THIS DOES NOT SAY, and it is the most likely misquote: an immaterial ALPHA cost is not a finding that the live absence is acceptable. The live product computes a DIFFERENT COMPOSITE from the one every published figure is measured on, which is a claims-integrity issue independent of return and the same class of defect audit B7 exists to prevent. Either build the sources or quote the headline for the book actually computed - screener lane's call. C3: the calibrated floors were measured at N=121 and re-verified at 129; adoption is monotone decreasing in N so an identical adopter set means identical scores and identical percentiles, and recomputing from the banked X7_RECONCILE margins gives 20 adopters at N=129 and 20 at N=135 with the set identical, so the floors still stand at this N. ONE SHIPPED-CODE CHANGE: quantile_backtest gained an OPT-IN return_series - it already computed the per-period draws and discarded them, so a paired comparison had no way to reach the shipped arithmetic and X3 had written a second implementation of the same series. Default payloads are bit-identical, pinned by test, and a further test pins the two implementations to agree. Equity N 131 -> 135, four arms; the halves are the same arms on subsets and are not charged, on session 7's precedent of charging 7 for 7 LOO arms measured both directions. |

ALL THREE SOURCES ARE FREE AND PUBLIC - the brief's premise is correct and is what makes this buildable: SF3 is a LICENSED AGGREGATION of 13F, the underlying filings are public record, and nothing here touches data/backtest. institutional from SEC Form 13F structured data sets (two quarterly zips, 31-DEC-2025 -> 31-MAR-2026, 90.3MB + 99.4MB, 22,092 and 22,626 CUSIPs from 8,625 and 8,741 distinct filers over 3.08M and 3.11M share rows), aggregated into holder breadth and SHARE-based accumulation and combined exactly as factors.py:267. capital_discipline from XBRL company-facts share issuance (factors.py:254 is neg_issuance ALONE). insider from the repo's ALREADY-FIXED Form 4 scraper, imported and called unmodified. THE BRIEF'S THIRD ASK LANDS IN A DIFFERENT THEME AND THE REGISTER SAID SO BEFORE THE RUN: accruals is a QUALITY input (factors.py:227), not a capital_discipline one, so net issuance is the only input that can revive that theme and accruals can only improve a theme that was never dead. Built and reported anyway, labelled against the right theme; coverage 385/500 (77.0pct), the shortfall being IFRS filers with no us-gaap Assets/CFO. RESULT, vs the 500 served rows of snapshot 2026-08-08: institutional 411 (82.2pct, 410 distinct), capital_discipline 456 (91.2pct, 441 distinct), insider 500 (100pct, 297 distinct against ONE today). Share of deployed weight reaching a live score, mean over 500 names: 56.5pct -> 95.5pct; 31 names still below 80pct. ALL FIVE PRE-COMMITTED BOUNDS HELD, including the falsifiable external-validity one: most-held served name NVDA at 5,775 distinct filers and Spearman(holder breadth, log market cap) +0.539. THE JOIN WAS THE HARD PART. 13F keys on CUSIP, the universe keys on ticker, and no free CUSIP master exists, so the key is a recorded two-rung ladder: the company's own SC 13D/G filings carry its CUSIP and every candidate must pass the mod-10 CHECK DIGIT (398 names), then normalised exact name match (13); 49 ambiguous and 28 unmatched are refusals, never coin flips. Ownership anchor (13F dollars / market cap) passed 423/423 with median 0.682 - a 68pct institutional-ownership median across a large-cap screen is the strongest single evidence the join landed on the right issuers. TWO DEFECTS FOUND IN MY OWN INSTRUMENT. (a) The CUSIP check digit valued letters off a '0-9A-Z' alphabet - off by ten - which silently rejected every CUSIP containing a letter, i.e. most ADRs; caught by a KSPI fixture before the run. (b) THE PRE-REGISTERED ANCHOR IS ONE-SIDED IN EFFECT: (0,1.50] rejects implausibly HIGH ownership and waves through implausibly LOW, so a join onto a stale CUSIP nobody reports holding lands at ~1e-6 INSIDE the band. It passed 12 names - CMCSA, RIO, BTI, HSBC, MT, AMP, CM, MGA, SHG, IHG, TELNY, GALDY, IFNNY - megacaps credited with ONE reporting institution, two of which returned null only by luck. Fixed with a STRUCTURAL floor MIN_HOLDERS=2 (sm_breadth is growth in holder count; one holder cannot express breadth or its change), recorded as a tightening with BOTH figures published: 421/500 pre-registered, 411/500 tightened. Four names at 2-5 holders remain suspicious and are named rather than removed by tuning. A THIRD CONTAMINATION, REFUSED SAFELY: a company that is ITSELF an asset manager files SC 13Gs ABOUT OTHER ISSUERS and EDGAR's submissions feed carries filings it MADE as well as those naming it as subject - PFG returned six candidate CUSIPs at one vote each and most_common(1) would have broken that tie by insertion order. 29 of 500 names hit a tie; a tie is now a refusal. Join failure runs 26.8pct inside Financial Services against 13.2pct outside. THE INSIDER THEME COMES ALIVE AND ITS SHAPE IS THE FINDING: median 43.68, 179 names (35.8pct) exactly 50.0, but 278 below 50 against only 43 above - as constructed it is overwhelmingly a 'who is selling least' sort, not a 'who is buying' one. 16 names pin at exactly 10.0, the 50+40*tanh(pressure/4000) FLOOR, which corroborates audit item S3's saturation mechanism on live data. RATE LIMITS, V2F's lesson transferred: the 13F leg BATCHES ALL THE WAY (two ~100MB zips replace tens of thousands of per-filer fetches, ~31s to aggregate), while the per-ticker legs are LATENCY-bound not quota-bound - one serial process reached ~3 req/s against SEC's ~10 req/s ceiling, so four interleaved shards took ~3 hours down to ~48 minutes. SEC pushed back 27 times across the fleet and NONE was recorded: the manifest refuses a non-terminal status by construction, so a throttled unit is retried and coverage cannot inflate by hitting a wall. Final 500/500 on all three legs. report makes ZERO network calls. PREDICTIONS 2 right, 2 wrong, 1 not evaluable (P3 wrong - capital_discipline coverage is HIGHER than institutional, not lower; P4's second half wrong - 35.8pct at exactly 50, not the predicted >=50pct). ADOPTION IS EXPLICITLY NOT LICENSED BY THIS: coverage is necessary, not sufficient. It needs the pipeline builder's cost measurement (the insider leg fetches EVERY Form 4 in the window with no cap; 23 of 500 names exceeded 40 filings), the held-out gate at the standing 100bps / 0.25 LS-t margins in BOTH directions, and acceptance that under Amendment 1 Rule 6 an adoption CLOSES VINTAGE 2, opens vintage 3 and resets the entire accrued forward clock for no statistical gain. Zero trial cost; equity N stays 131. 5 BUGS FOUND. |

| LA12 | LA | sector median_upside was computed over a stale subset while count reported the whole sector | **DONE** | FIXED - the median now carries its own denominator |  | HANDOFF_live_data_bugs.md Part 16 | 2026-08-11 | human | /api/hotstocks calls sector_attractiveness(all_rows) on rows straight from the database, BEFORE estimate_fair_values has run on anything - that runs only on the served slice. Only names given a full DCF carry an `upside`, and production runs SCAN_DCF_TOP=12 over the whole market, so _median (which drops Nones silently) returned a median over one or two names sitting beside a `count` reporting the entire sector: two populations in one row. FIX: ship median_upside_n beside it, so the number can never be read against the wrong denominator again. NO FLOOR WAS INVENTED and that was deliberate - a threshold here would be an uncalibrated constant, whereas a denominator lets the reader apply their own. `count` was NOT changed: it was never wrong, it was being read against a median that meant something else, and 'fixing' it would break the correct field to hide the symptom. Pinned by the invariant that median_upside_n is 0 exactly when median_upside is None. Nothing renders this today (app.js reads avg_composite only), which is the reason to fix it while it is still cheap rather than the reason to leave it. |
| LA14 | LA | market_holidays(y) could return a date in year y-1 | **DONE** | FIXED - the set contains only dates in the year it names |  | HANDOFF_live_data_bugs.md Part 16 | 2026-08-11 | human | _observed(date(y,1,1)) moves a Saturday New Year's Day to the PRECEDING Friday, which is 31 December of y-1. Measured before the fix: market_holidays(2028) contained 2027-12-31 and market_holidays(2033) contained 2032-12-31. DROPPING IT IS THE FACTUALLY CORRECT NYSE RULE, NOT MERELY TIDINESS: the exchange does not close on 31 December when 1 January falls on a Saturday, so the holiday is not observed at all - and the neighbouring year must not gain it either, which is checked (market_holidays(2027) does not contain 2027-12-31 and is_trading_day(2027-12-31) is True). It was inert for is_trading_day, which asks market_holidays(d.year) and so never saw the stray, and inert CORRECTLY for the same reason; the exposure is to any caller that ITERATES the set rather than testing membership, which would receive a date outside the year it asked for while the year that date belongs to silently lacked it. Nothing iterates it today - this closes the hole before something does. The raw computation is split into _holidays_unfiltered so the filter is visible and testable rather than folded into the comprehension that corrects it, and a test asserts the filter actually REMOVED something (a guard that passes because it never had anything to catch is not a guard) plus that ordinary years are untouched and no year from 1999 to 2060 leaks a date. |
| LA10 | LA | A withheld row keeps the labels that described the value it withheld | **DONE** | FIXED 2026-08-10 |  | HANDOFF_appfixes.md session 24 | 2026-08-10 | app-fixer | THE SHAPE, NOT THE SIZE, IS WHY THIS WAS WORTH FIXING. `estimate_fair_values` writes `fair_value_method` ("blended") and `fair_value_confidence` ("medium") beside the number; `withhold.withhold_implausible_fair_values` then blanked `fair_value` and `upside` and left both labels standing, so a refused row shipped as `{fair_value: null, fair_value_method: "blended", fair_value_confidence: "medium", fair_value_withheld: true}` - the confidence of a number that is not in the payload. That is the same "a label outlives the value it described" shape `withhold.py` exists to eliminate, one level up: the original KSPI bug was a FIGURE surviving its own suppression, this is the DESCRIPTION of it surviving. FIXED AS ONE DEFINITION, NOT TWO EDITS: `publication.strip_derived_fields` is the single rule for what a refusal does to a row, and both refusal paths call it - the scan-side `record_refusal` and the web-side band withhold. Two places decide a row is withheld; there is now one definition of what that does, for the same reason that module owns the band. THE TWO FIELDS ARE TREATED DIFFERENTLY ON PURPOSE: `fair_value_method` is SET to "withheld" (already the project's vocabulary - `fairvalue.py`'s own refusal branch writes it and test_guards.py:316 pins it - and a positive label beats an empty cell, the same argument as writing a refusal REASON rather than leaving a gap), while `fair_value_confidence` is CLEARED, because it is a graded scale with no withheld rung and a word in a scale invites a renderer to rank it. NO VISIBLE CHANGE, AND THAT IS THE CORRECT OUTCOME: `app.js:1039` tests `fair_value_withheld` and returns "withheld" BEFORE it ever reads the method, so the page has always shown the right thing - the WIRE was what disagreed with it. The audit's "cosmetic today, a future renderer would pick it up" is confirmed exactly. PINNED BY EXTENDING THE CATCH-ALL, WHICH WAS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO IT: `_walk_fair_values` walked ratios, and a withheld row has no ratio, so every band assertion passed on precisely the rows carrying the stale labels. It now carries the two labels too, and `test_no_public_api_response_describes_a_fair_value_it_withheld` asserts a marked row has no live method or confidence anywhere in a public body, with a non-vacuity floor of >=2 withheld rows so it cannot become a test of nothing. Mutation-verified 3/3: the bug as reported, and BOTH half-fixes (method left standing; confidence left standing) - each caught on the real production AEG row (5.25x, $49.91 against $9.50). test_withhold 30/30; full gate 37 suites, every one exit 0. |
| LA13 | LA | surfaces.py claims every /api route is classified, and the exemption lived in the test | **DONE** | FIXED 2026-08-10 - and the gap under it was bigger than the claim |  | HANDOFF_appfixes.md session 24 | 2026-08-10 | app-fixer | THE AUDIT FOUND A DOCSTRING GAP; UNDERNEATH IT WAS AN UNTESTED GUARD. `surfaces.py:63` promised "every registered /api route is knowingly on one side or the other - a new route lands in neither list and fails the suite", and `test_public.py:127` skipped `/api/option-alerts/` with a hard-coded prefix. The exemption is correct (both handlers call `_admin_ok()`), but it lived in the TEST, so the module stating the policy had no record that a third category existed, and a future admin-token route under a different prefix would have fallen through to public by default. FIXED BY WRITING THE THIRD SIDE DOWN: `ADMIN_TOKEN_PREFIXES`, derived from `private.ADMIN_PREFIXES` rather than restated (the list that lets these routes through the lockdown must be the list that classifies them here), plus `is_admin_token()` and `classify()` as the single reader. The suite now walks `classify` instead of keeping a second copy of the policy. THE FINDING THIS PRODUCED, WHICH THE AUDIT DID NOT NAME: no test anywhere asserted that these two endpoints REFUSE an un-tokened caller. The four references to them in the suite were a source-string check (test_intraday:327), an assertion that the lockdown lets them through (test_private:145, `always_open` is True - reachability, not refusal), and the two skips. Deleting `_admin_ok()` from a handler would have been caught by nothing. Classifying a route secures nothing, and a category is strictly WORSE than a hard-coded skip if it becomes a one-line way to move a route out of the public set while enforcing nothing - so `test_the_admin_token_category_is_not_a_way_to_leave_a_route_unguarded` calls every admin-token route for real, with no token, on every verb it accepts, and requires 401/403. Mutation-verified 3/3: dropping the category from `classify` (both routes report unclassified), dropping `_admin_ok()` from the handler (GET /api/option-alerts/open answered **200** to an anonymous caller - the one that matters), and drifting `private.ADMIN_PREFIXES` apart from the surfaces list. ALSO SWEPT, OUTSIDE THE ITEM'S WORDING: `/admin/*` is not classified by surfaces.py at all (it only speaks about /api), so the walk cannot see it and the same untested-guard gap applied. The enforcement pin was already generic, so `test_every_admin_route_refuses_a_caller_with_no_token` extends it. Measured first, then pinned: all 13 route-verbs ALREADY answer 401 with a real ADMIN_TOKEN set - the case that means anything, since an unset token fails closed trivially - so this pins a property the app had rather than reporting a hole. test_public 31/31 (was 27); full gate 37 suites, every one exit 0. |

But the live sources are raw-EDGAR APPROXIMATIONS of the panel's licensed SF2/SF3 themes, and wiring a DIFFERENT theme under a validated theme's name is the B7 disease - it would not fix the coherence problem, it would HIDE it. THE BAR, FIXED BEFORE MEASURING: Spearman(live, panel) per theme on the panel's last cross-section against max(0.60 floor, P95 of abs rho between DIFFERENT panel themes). The calibrated half is the X7 method applied to a correlation and is computed from the panel ALONE, so no fidelity result informed it; over 36 distinct panel-theme pairs the median abs rho is 0.1878 and P95 is 0.3590, so the bar bound at the 0.60 floor. THE DIAGNOSES WERE BOTH MY CONSTRUCTION, NOT THE SOURCE. institutional: the formula matched and both inputs diverged - the panel's _inst_accum reads totalvalue FIRST, i.e. DOLLARS, while my build used SHARES, and my own test pinned that choice with the reasoning that dollars would make it a momentum signal in a 13F coat. That argument is sound and is exactly why the column was not the panel's: the panel's quantity deliberately CONTAINS the price move. I built a better signal and a different one. And the quarters were two periods apart - the panel at 2026-01-28 uses 30-SEP-2025 vs 30-JUN-2025 at its 45-day lag where I used 31-MAR-2026 vs 31-DEC-2025, with no overlap at all. insider: a different estimator plus a fabricated neutral - the panel sums SIGNED DOLLARS unweighted and counts positive TRANSACTIONS, the live scorer sums code-weighted role-weighted SQRT of value and counts buying FILINGS, and the code and role weighting is not a monotone transform so it moves ranks; and the panel returns None on an empty window where the live scorer returns 50.0, which had put 179 of 500 names into ONE TIE BLOCK against panel values that are absent entirely. THE REBUILD REMOVED THE CONFOUND THE FIRST GATE CARRIED: both themes were re-measured on the PANEL'S OWN WINDOW - the aligned quarters and a date-aligned Form 4 crawl over 2025-10-30 to 2026-01-28, 5,639 documents across 500 names - so these are source-to-source comparisons rather than source-plus-drift, which is what makes the verdict mean something in either direction. THREE BUGS FOUND DURING THE REBUILD, all before they could corrupt a number. (1) V2G's dataset reader assumed a FLAT zip; SEC packs 01sep2025-30nov2025 flat and 01jun2025-31aug2025 nested under a directory, so opening by bare name raised KeyError and killed the run - it never bit V2G because both quarters it happened to need are flat, and a reader that works on the two archives you first tried is not a reader that works on the format. (2) My aggregation stored the WRONG LEVEL - aggregate_13f returns a wrapper of period/shape/cusips and I saved the wrapper, so it printed 3 CUSIPs, literally its three keys, against V2G's 22,000; the absurdity of the number is the only reason it was caught before the join silently produced an empty column and the gate scored noise. (3) The live Form 4 parser exposes only UNSIGNED value_usd and recovers direction through CODE_WEIGHTS, so summing it would have made net a GROSS TURNOVER figure, a third distinct quantity; a signed parser reading transactionAcquiredDisposedCode was written and unit-checked before the crawl ran. WHAT SHIPS AND HOW: the gate proved the ESTIMATOR, not the window, so production applies the same estimator to the CURRENT window - the latest two complete 13F periods and the trailing 90 days - exactly as the panel applied it to whatever was current at each of its own dates, and a separate 6,133-document crawl built today's cache. valuation/screener/live_themes.py reads that pre-built cache and makes NO network calls, because building 5,600 SEC requests into a scan would put them on the critical path; it FAILS TO None on every path so a missing or stale cache costs coverage rather than correctness, and staleness is REPORTED rather than tolerated with a 120-day refusal since 13F periods roll quarterly. Measured end to end on 60 names: institutional 0 to 26 non-null with 26 distinct, insider 1 to 20 distinct - it had been the constant 0.0 that factors.py:284 writes when insider_score is absent, which is why a 100pct-covered column carried no information. Served coverage: inst_accum 82.2pct, sm_breadth 82.2pct, insider_score 72.6pct. THE VINTAGE WAS AMENDED, NOT RE-OPENED, AND THE RULE WAS REGISTERED FIRST: the registers were SILENT on staged restoration, so section 4 of ef765fc registered the interpretation before any number existed - an adopted change made while the current vintage has accrued ZERO complete days AMENDS that vintage instead of opening the next. It is faithful rather than convenient because Rule 6 protects a CLOCK and there was none to protect, because the shadow's predecessor stays vintage 2 (the four-theme book the comparison is actually about) where opening vintage 4 would shadow a book that never ran a single day, and because it is bounded by a FACT rather than a judgement - one accrued day and Amendment 1's ordinary rule resumes. VERIFIED CONSEQUENCES: vintage 3's opening date is unchanged at 2026-08-11 and the gate stays 2027-02-11 and the verdict 2031-08-11, so THE CLOCK WAS NOT RESET A SECOND TIME; themes_scored_live goes from five to all SEVEN weighted themes; vintage 3's params_id DOES move, 441531f1de2b to 24878e43a1e3, because the model genuinely changed, recorded openly because Part 17 published the old one; vintage 2's pin is untouched at 0060c5ef3dda; no fourth vintage, one shadow pair, still live 3 shadow 2. NOT DONE: the live insider SCORER is unchanged - screener/insider.py keeps its code-weighted statistic for its own surfaces and the panel-matching one is computed in the cache builder, since changing the shipped scorer would be a separate adoption needing its own gate; and there is NO automated refresh of the cache, which is the open item this leaves. Expectations 2 of 3 right, and the wrong one was insider, predicted to fail at 35/65. Zero trial cost, equity N unchanged. 3 BUGS FOUND. |

WHY +10pp IS THE WRONG BAR, ARGUED FROM THE DISTRIBUTION RATHER THAN FROM WANTING TO PASS. Attainability, measured over all 33 exit arms ever scored on the 3,885-trade book: exactly ONE clears +10pp, tp100_only at +10.78pp, and it FAILS paired-cell FDR, lifts tail concentration from 86.75% to 92.75%, and per inventory item J fails the name-year sign test on the alert book at z -5.76 while passing on random at +10.55 - so the bar's rejection region over this family holds one point and it is occupied by the one arm the record already refuses. A bar only a discredited arm can clear is not selecting for quality; on this family it selects for removing the stop. THE UNIT IS THE REAL PROBLEM AND LOWERING THE NUMBER DOES NOT FIX IT: this book's expectancy is a tail statistic - hit rate 35.3%, median trade -52.2%, 86.75% of gross winnings from the tail - and the GAINS are worse, with the top 1% of trades carrying 106% to 210% of each arm's entire gain, meaning the other 99% are collectively negative. THE OBVIOUS FIX IS REFUTED BY MEASUREMENT AND IT IS THE ONE I EXPECTED TO ENDORSE: switching to a RELATIVE bar because the payoff is multiplicative fails on the two books we have - between look 1 and look 2 the absolute gain drifts +17% (tp200) and +52% (tp150) while the relative gain drifts +62% and +109%, because the baseline expectancy itself fell -27.6%, so the ABSOLUTE figure is two to four times the more stable unit. Reported because it cuts against adopting. The bar is also an order of magnitude from the design's own resolution: month-block clustered SEs on the paired gain run 0.29pp to 2.10pp, resolving ~0.6-4.2pp at t=2. WHAT THE MEMO PRE-REGISTERS IS A PROCEDURE, NOT A NUMBER, deliberately - any level argued in prose would be one chosen after seeing which arms pass, and X7 is the precedent for calibrating instead: C1 the bar is the p95 of a null built by jittering exit parameters, n=100, seeds 1000-1099, identical paths and scorer, BINDING whatever it returns including a bar above +3.82pp; C2 a condition that does not run through the mean - the gain must survive winsorising the top 1% of per-trade differences, a real hazard given the concentration table; C3 entry-independence, already satisfied and corroborated by the path study's r 0.967 slope 0.990 across 13 arms; C4 the existing FDR, both-halves, both-sets and PBO gates. No new market data is needed. TIMING FACT MOST LIKELY TO BE ASSUMED WRONG: the paper options book holds three positions all OPEN and ZERO closed trades, so a change now breaks no forward-record continuity - which makes adopt CHEAP, not RIGHT, and expires with the first closed trade; and no vintage closes, checked not assumed, because the contract binds the published Index and not this book. WHAT THE REJECTION DOES AND DOES NOT SAY, recorded because it is easy to over-read: it does NOT say raising the take-profit does nothing - every other condition says the effect is real, broad and entry-independent. It says the arm is not DISTINGUISHED WITHIN ITS OWN FAMILY, because 53 of 100 arbitrary jitters beat the shipped rule and the best five beat it by +5.2 to +8.1pp, all the same wider-stop-higher-target-longer-hold shape; when a whole REGION looks good on one corpus the corpus cannot say where inside it the optimum is, and picking one point after five looks is choosing by hindsight. BY-PRODUCT UNFLATTERING TO THE INHERITED RULE, reported because it cuts that way: the shipped +100%/-50%/half-DTE exit is slightly BELOW the median of its own family (53/100 beat it, +0.803pp at the median), so it is an ordinary member rather than a local optimum - and it CANNOT be acted on for precisely the reason tp150 was refused, so nobody may later present the shipped exit as validated by this exercise. | LA8 | LA | "Days" on the forward-track cards is a row count presented as trading days | **DONE** | FIXED 2026-08-11 - and the number it was hiding is a recording failure, not a short record |  | HANDOFF_appfixes.md session 26 | 2026-08-11 | app-fixer | OUT-OF-BAND (VALQUO_LIVE_AUDIT.md cold audit, not one of the 134); build_ledger.py regenerates from the 134 audit ids only and will DROP this row. THE DEFECT IS A FALSE SENTENCE IN THE FLATTERING DIRECTION, WHICH IS WHY IT WAS WORTH MORE THAN A LABEL FIX. Every surface rendered `len(series)` - rows the recorder managed to write - under the word "Days", beside "Alpha / yr" and "Sharpe", and the server's own note read 'Live track is 2 trading days old - far too short to judge' while the track was 7 trading days old. The reader is told THE RECORD IS SHORT; the true statement is THE RECORDER IS MISSING 71% OF ITS ROWS - a different problem, a different owner (PT-WRITER, Cowork lane), and one somebody might act on. The single number that would have made the recording failure visible was being spent to say something else. THERE ARE THREE CLOCKS AND THE FIX IS TO NAME ALL THREE, not to swap one for another: ROWS gate ('have we recorded enough to say anything?'), ELAPSED-TO-LAST-ROW annualises (LA3's fix, the window the return accrued over), and AGE displays (inception -> today). valuation/screener/track_age.py is the third, and it is deliberately NOT a restatement of the second: `_elapsed_trading_days` stops at the last recorded row, so a recorder that died three weeks ago leaves it FROZEN and the track appears to stop ageing at the moment it stopped being written - the most flattering failure mode available, and invisible in every other field. Age runs to today, so a dead recorder widens a visible gap instead of going quiet. Pinned by test_the_default_clock_is_today_and_not_the_last_recorded_row, which exercises the PRODUCTION default (every other test passes `today` explicitly and would pass with the defect restored - the mutation harness found that, not reasoning). THE GATE DID NOT MOVE AND THAT IS LOAD-BEARING: MIN_LIVE_DAYS still counts recorded rows, because moving it onto elapsed time would let a gappy track reach the floor sooner and advance the public backtested->live posture on days nobody recorded. LA3 recorded that reasoning; this change is where it could quietly have been undone. `thin` alone cannot detect the move (the contract gate is independently unpassed, so `thin` stays true either way) - the NOTE's branch is the only observable that separates the two rules, and the test asserts on it. SIX SURFACES, ONE SOURCE: the three server notes, the hero band (index.html), the landing page, and two sites in app.js. All read the same `age` dict off the payload; the card is pinned against computing its own (no `new Date(`, no `Date.parse`, no `86400` beside it), so the card, the band, the landing page and the server cannot disagree about how old the track is. Rows are still shown - as a SECOND number, 'Recorded', appearing only when it differs from the age - because hiding the row count would be the same defect with the other number. THE GAPLESS CASE IS BYTE-IDENTICAL, which is what makes it safe to land: on a track written every trading day, recorded == age and phrase() returns exactly the string the old f-string built, pinned across n = 1/2/5/60/252. So the display changes only where it was wrong. CONTRACT POSTURE UNTOUCHED, pinned verbatim: 'It is shown for transparency, not as evidence, and the headline stays on the backtest' and 'Elapsed time alone does not promote a live number'. The one posture word that DID change is the floor's unit - '60-day floor' -> '60-recorded-day floor', and app.js 'withheld until N trading days' -> 'N RECORDED trading days' - because leaving the unit ambiguous beside a now-correct age re-creates the exact conflation the item names. 22 tests; 16/16 mutations caught, each by the test that names it, including the three that initially MISSED (a duplicated literal 0 that made the negative-gap clamp untestable; the default-clock gap above; and the gate move hiding behind `thin`) - all three were real holes in the pins and were fixed rather than argued away. Full gate 46 suites, 0 non-zero, 1160/1161 assertions; the single shortfall is test_guards.py's pre-existing declared XFAIL (options-bot lane, exit 0, unchanged by this work).
